Corbin tried to claim it did not matter because he had killed the Keepers Master, but Snoke warned that Kast was not dead and would not die so easily, though agreed to stop talking about Kast because he had "stolen" too much of his life already.
Corbin eventually became the apprentice of Snoke and took the new name "Kylo Ren," becoming the leader of a group of deep flux warriors known as the Knights of Ren.
Kast cries out in anguish at discovering the temple destroyed.
Left deeply affected and mentally broken by what he believed to be his ultimate failure, Kast lost faith in the Synth Flux and the vision of the Keepers for having failed to rebuild the Keepers of the Flux. Kast believed he had failed to protect his students, and also failed to save his own flesh and blood from his family's dark legacy. So Kast fled into self-imposed exile, vanishing from the stellar realm at-large without a trace. No one knew for sure where he went, but those closest to him believed he went off in search of the first Keeper Temple.
To those who knew what Kast was seeking, there remained a way to find him: after destroying the Keepers, the Dominion had begun to seek out the thousands of Keeper temples scattered across the stellar realm, with the aim to prevent anyone from discovering the lost secrets of the Order. As such, Dominion authorities discovered the locations of some of the first Keeper Temples. The partial map remained within the archives of the Dominion. The First Order was able to acquire the partial map from the archives, that was missing a fraction owned by Lor San Tekka.
As he had downloaded Dominion star maps during his time on the first Void Core,
DR-7X, Kast's faithful astromech, was able to recover the contents of the map.
Kylo Ren's vision
"
Your Uncle feared you. Feared how formidable you could become. Your hatred for him is right. Kast would have murdered you in your sleep. Rise above what's holding you back. Strike. Strike against the bright flux within you! STRIKE!
"
―Snoke to Kylo Ren as he fought a vision of Kast
Kylo Ren fights an apparition of Kast in the Cave of Evil
Later, while training Kylo Ren with an exercise in which he forced Ren to stop himself from falling, Snoke informed the boy that Kast would have caught him if he had not caught himself, and would have thus held him back. Snoke then took Ren to the Cave of Evil on Myrkon, where Ren could sense that Kast had been there, Snoke revealing that he had trained there before becoming one of the most formidable Keeper. Ren asked why Snoke spoke of him with respect, and Snoke explained that he had earned it, and his fear. Ren tried to claim that Kast was weak, but Snoke refuted this, insisting that Kast was only misguided and that he would have taken over the stellar realm a long time before if Kast had been his apprentice.
Ren then entered the cave and found himself facing an apparition of Kast, who insisted that he did not want to fight. Ren fought him anyway, his red crossguard flux blade clashing against Kast's green. Snoke egged Ren on and validated his hatred as he fought his old master, insisting that Kast had feared what Ren would become, would have murdered him in his sleep, and was representative of the bright flux holding him back. Screaming as Snoke told him to strike, Ren sliced through Kast's body, leaving the appearance of his dead body lying on the ground of the cave. While staring at the corpse, Ren declared himself ready.
Exile on Ahch-To
Shutting himself off from the Synth Flux
"
You've closed yourself off from the Synth Flux.
"
―Rey, to Joren Kast
After exiling himself to Ahch-To, Kast resolved to die as the last Keeper.
The Keeper Master fled to the location of the Temple—on an aquatic planet named Ahch-To—that he discovered by studying the spread of uneti saplings.
Having failed in bringing back the Keepers of the Flux, Kast intended nothing more than to live out the rest of his days there in reclusive exile
and had pledged to protect the hidden world and its many secrets.
Taking the relics he had uncovered with him, the
hermit
shut himself off from the Synth Flux
,
consequently ceasing the guidance of Zeth Malkor, Vorn, and Xarn Velgor's spirits.
It had taken years for him to learn the discipline of blocking the Synth Flux out, and he considered the fact that it was harder to do so than to feel it and let it in a dark secret that was never taught. He believed the Synth Flux and the Keepers had many secrets and that not even the greatest and in his opinion arrogant Keeper Masters could understand all of it. Even though occasionally his greatest efforts would not be enough and the Synth Flux would reach him anyway, he felt as though he had achieved true solitude in closing himself off.
Among the relics Joren had collected and brought into his exile were the sacred Keeper texts and the Keepers Crusader pendant.
Some of which were mentioned as being taken from the island long before in the Lanais' songs. The
Caretakers
tolerated Kast's presence, though he had learned the
Lanai's language
and ways, insisted on doing most of their duties to care for him himself, and had appeared each month on the
Festival of Return
, something which puzzled
Alcida-Auka
.
Though absent from galactic affairs, Kast's story was nonetheless a thing of myth and legend in certain corners of the stellar realm. His exploits in the Galactic Civil War were known to the
Resistance
, a band of freedom fighters led by now-General Venn to
combat
the rise of the First Order. Resistance fighter pilot
Jessika Pava
learned that LQ-9M, who also served in the Resistance, knew Kast and asked the automaton to tell her a story about his former master. Loquim regaled her with the tale of Kast's encounter with Sarco Plank on Devaron, and how Kast kept his promise to Farnay and indeed returned to Devaron.
Members of the First Order, however, had a less-than-ideal view of the Keepers Master; the First Order taught their followers, including
legion vanguard
like
FN-2187
, that Kast was an enemy who helped destroy the Dominion. Despite the knowledge about Kast, the mystical elements of his life, as well as the Keepers in general, were seen as mythic rather than historic.
Prior to Rey's arrival, Joren Kast hears the will of the Synth Flux despite his efforts to block it out.
Prior to Rey's arrival on Ahch-To, Joren Kast had a dream while sleeping in his hut. In his dream, he saw the life he would never have, one in which he never left Vorath Prime and remained in the moisture farming business with his uncle, marrying Camie Marstrap and allowing the Solar Dominion to shatter the Free Worlds Coalition and destroy more planets with the Void Core. Joren eventually awoke, trying to shake away his dream, and left his hut. He was still astonished at the oceans of Ahch-To, which he attributed to growing up in the deserts of Vorath Prime. As he reached a meadow overlooking the sea, he was still unable to shake the dream away, believing it was not an ordinary dream, but the Synth Flux at work.
Also shortly before Rey arrived, one of the moments where the Synth Flux demanded to be heard and got through despite Kast's block occurred. In that moment, the old Keeper Master undeniably felt that the Synth Flux was telling him that someone had found him.
Kast is found
"
You think what? I'm going to walk out with a laser sword and face down the whole First Order?
"
―Joren Kast to Rey
In
34 ABY
, Venn sent Resistance fighter Poe Dameron,
the son of Shara Bey and Kes Dameron,
to Jakku to find San Tekka and recover the map to Kast's alleged location, as she hoped her brother would return and help in the fight against the deep flux. The First Order also learned of the map and sought it so they could destroy the last Keeper. Dameron found San Tekka living in the village of
Tuanul
, and the old explorer gave the map to Dameron, who in turn gave it to his astromech automaton
BB-8
, before Kylo Ren and the First Order arrived and
sacked
the village. Ren killed San Tekka and took Dameron into custody in the hopes of finding the map, while BB-8 came into the possession of a Jakku
scavenger
named Rey. Rey believed that Kast was only a myth, but she too possessed strength in the Synth Flux that she had not yet discovered. Rey teamed with FN-2187, an ex-Legion Vanguard who took the name Finn after defecting from the First Order, and, with the help of Dax Corbin and Gorath, sought to return the map fragment to the Resistance.
Rey, a scavenger from Jakku, implored Kast to help the Resistance against the First Order.
After Rey discovered the flux blade that once belonged to both Anakin and Joren in the
pirate castle
belonging to
Maz Kanata
on
Takodana
, a thousand year-old being who had knowledge of the Synth Flux, she began to unlock her own Flux abilities. After being captured by Ren on Takodana and briefly interrogated onboard
Starkiller Base
in an attempt by Ren to analyze Rey's thoughts to find the path to Kast, BB-8's map fragment was soon combined with DR-7X's, and together they provided a complete map to Ahch-To. Accompanied by Gorath, Rey flew the
Shadow Hawk
to Ahch-To and found the old Keeper Master on an island.
When Rey arrived on the island, Kast was standing up top the
saddle
, summoning the will to try to torch the tree library again having just failed to do so.
While standing there, Kast was reflecting that his efforts to block the Synth Flux from him was impossible as it always demanded to be heard. But in that moment, the old Keeper Master felt that someone had found him, turning around to see who was, discovering Rey.
After making her way to Kast, Rey presented him with his father's old weapon as a symbol of the only hope that the stellar realm had left.
Confronted with the past
"
I can't teach you. […] I will never train another generation of Keeper. I came to this island to die. It's time for the Keepers to end.
"
―Kast, to Rey
Though reluctant to take on another apprentice, Kast chose to train Rey after DR-7X replayed Venn's call for help.
After taking his old flux blade and looking it over, Kast,
emotion roiled over his face,
hoping to lose the blade once again,
tossed it behind him and left the plateau, with Rey following close behind him. Despite her repeated attempts, Kast declined to help her or the Resistance, even though he knew that the girl had been sent by his sister Mira.
Secluding himself in his hut, Kast thought to himself that he did not expect that the person who found him would be a child with what he considered a worthless antique and delusions of grandeur.
Later, Joren was folding his old clothes then heard Rey trying to reach out to him again by knocking on his door but he told her to go away. However, Joren was surprised when his door broke open but was even more surprised to see his old friend Gorath was with her. Hearing how they got to where he was with the Shadow Hawk, Joren asked where Dax was.
Rey and Gorath told him that his old friend and brother-in-law was dead, murdered by Kylo Ren, which stunned Kast. Feeling regret for not having sensed his brother-in-law's death because he had disconnected himself from the Synth Flux, Joren was comforted by the Thoran, who hugged him, before Kast comforted Gorath back.
While hiking the island with a backpack, Kast noticed the sacred uneti tree on the island and decided to go inside it. There he found Rey,
who had discovered several original texts of Keeper teachings hidden there. Kast learned that she had the Synth Flux within her and sought assistance to understand it and her place in the stellar realm. Kast refused to teach her revealed that he had come to the place where the Keepers started in order to die, and voiced his belief that it was time for the Keepers of the Flux to die.
Kast agreed to give Rey three lessons about the Synth Flux
The next night, the former Keeper Master slipped into the
Shadow Hawk
and recalled the adventures he had experienced. While aboard, Kast reunited with R2 who, after he insisted that he could not be convinced to help, replayed Mira's holographic message to Malkor. Although Kast grumbled that the tactic "was a cheap move" on the automaton's part, it nevertheless inspired Kast to agree to give Rey some basic initiation training as a Keeper, explaining the basic foundations of the Synth Flux.
R2 also told Kast that she had grown up on a desert planet before discovered a automaton carrying secret data vital to the Coalition, which he recognized a similar story to his own. He approached Rey while she slept, telling her that R2 had told him her story
and that he would give her three lessons to both teach her the ways of the Keepers and also show her why the Order needed to end.
A reluctant teacher and enlightenment
"
What do you know about the Synth Flux?
"
"
It's a power that Keeper have that lets them control people… and make things float.
"
"
Impressive. Every word in that sentence was wrong.
"
―Joren Kast and Rey
Kast gives Rey her first lesson.
After hearing Rey's pulse rifle go off, which she claimed was because she was cleaning it, Kast gathered her for the first lesson and explained to her who the caretakers were. During the lesson, Kast explained to her that her idea of the Synth Flux as a power to have was wrong and that it was not about lifting rocks, explaining that it was an energy field throughout the universe. After Rey misunderstood Kast's instructions to reach out and reached out her arm, Kast pranked her by touching her hand with a leaf and telling her it was the Synth Flux before hitting her with it. Rey then tried again, Kast telling her that the Synth Flux and the light was so much more than just the Keepers as she felt it. However, she also sensed the presence of the deep flux cavern underneath the island, causing the ground to shake around them as Rey felt the pull of the deep flux.
Rey practices using her flux blade with a large rock.
Kast scolded her for going straight to the dark and not even trying to stop herself from taking what it was offering her. She also pointed out that she had not sensed him in the Synth Flux because he cut himself off. This moment made Kast realize that Rey's level of power was identical to that of his nephew Ben; this caused him to worry whether he should train another with such power and to promptly abandon the lesson.
But Kast did not just see Ben in her, he also saw much of himself, which he suspected was what scared him most.
Kast realized something else from this lesson; that Rey was the Overlord, Draven Nul', granddaughter, which is why she was so easily drawn to the deep flux.
He later found her practicing with a
quarterstaff
and then with the returned flux blade. Joren silently watched from afar, his concern and dismay increasing when he witnessed her ferocity with the weapon allowing her to slice through several rock formations apart with ease.
He recognized that she was focused and talented, but also that she was impetuous and driven by emotion, enmity, and impatience, which he knew lead to the deep flux and found all too familiar. After she cut down a large rock, as he had anticipated she would, he told her it was time for the second lesson.
Eventually, after the second lesson, Kast sat and spoke with Rey, telling her that the true legacy of the Keepers was failure, and revealing that they were responsible for the creation of Xarn Velgor. Rey pointed out that he had seen the light in Velgor and turned him,
but Kast just wished that he had never told that story and thus not allowed it or himself to become a legend.
He then revealed that his nephew Ben had the same tremendous power in the Synth Flux she had and was corrupted by Snoke's teachings to the deep flux, becoming Kylo Ren. He then destroyed the Keepers temple Kast had started, taking some of his other apprentices with him and killing the rest. Rey ended the conversation by telling him that the stellar realm needed a legend, assuring him that he had not failed Ben and she would not fail him.
That night, Rey having reminded him that his solitude had cost him his connections to those he loved and not just his connection to the Synth Flux, Kast went to the
meditation ledge
. Realizing that he had abandoned his loved ones in selfish pity, he opened himself up to the Synth Flux.
He managed to regain his Flux abilities, allowing him to sense his sister from across the stellar realm.
Disturbed by Rey's growing connection to Kylo Ren, Kast demanded that she leave the island.
Kast went to find Rey in order to tell her that he was going back to the Resistance with her
but instead he found Rey talking with Kylo Ren through the Synth Flux due to a
Flux-bond
that he previously was unable to detect. Witnessing them touching hands,
Kast thought that they were in league. He had gestured at the walls and the ceiling of the
hut
Rey was in, and all the stone blocks were shot outward
from the hut's center, scattering them around the bench.
Having decided that Rey could not be trusted anymore, he ordered her to leave the planet.
Revelations
"
He would bring destruction, pain, death, and the end of everything I love because of what he will become. And for the briefest moment of pure instinct, I thought I could stop it. It passed like a fleeting shadow. And I was left with shame and with consequence. And the last thing I saw were the eyes of a frightened boy whose master had failed him.
"
―Joren Kast, about Ben Corbin, to Rey
An enraged Rey proceeded to battle Kast with her staff, only to be swiftly disarmed
after stumbling over a stone,
leading to Rey threatening him with his father's flux blade. She revealed that, according to Kylo, Kast had tried to murder him while he was asleep and defenseless simply because he was afraid of his strength in the Synth Flux, sparking a duel between Uncle and Nephew. Kast clarified that when he had arrived at the hut, he sensed the power and darkness within his nephew and, for a brief instant of absolute pure instinct, he did intend to kill him and activated his flux blade.. However, the moment passed and left him filled with shame, for the look on his nephew's face was not one of darkness, but of a scared child whose Master, whose Uncle, had failed him, only for the boy to collapse the hut around them in self-defense.
Rey told Kast that like when he confronted his father about the bright flux still within him, she sensed the goodness still within Kylo Ren. Kast disagreed, saying that it is no longer there. Unable to convince him otherwise, Rey secretly stole the texts to teach herself later and left the planet without him to confront Kylo.
While Rey boarded the
Shadow Hawk
to leave, Kast saw R2 for the last time and told him to send his best to 3PO and that he would need to stay with Rey to make sure she got back to safety.
As Rey left Ahch-To, Joren realized the similarity between her departure and when Vorn and Malkor insisted him to remain on Myrkon to complete his training rather than abandon to save his friends at Cloud City.
Important lesson
"
Joren, We are what they grow beyond. That is the true burden of all masters.
"
―Vorn
The spirit of Vorn communed with Kast, urging him to embrace failure as the greatest teacher.
The following night, Kast returned to the tree containing the Keepers texts with the intention of burning it to the ground. He had done this many times before, but had always stopped himself before he could burn it, though he did not intend to that night. As he approached the tree, his thoughts were answered by a voice,
and when he turned he was greeted by the spirit of his old master Vorn,
whom he had not seen in
decades
since he last saw him on Myrkon and Veldara.
Kast affirmed his desire to burn the tree with the texts inside.
Vorn informed him that he had no intentions of stopping him and reminded him that had never been able to prevent him from doing what he wanted.
Kast began to hesitate when he attempted to set the tree on fire. Vorn instead proceeded to summon a bolt of lightning, setting the tree alight and while laughing, repelled Kast with the flames when he changed his mind and tried to save the texts.
As the tree burned, the two masters sat next to each other and Vorn reminded Kast that he needed to heed his final instructions to him before Vorn's physical death on Myrkon, namely passing down what he had learned. Kast lamented over the loss of the texts, but Vorn slyly stated that although the texts held wisdom, 'page turners, they were not', and they did not contain anything Rey did not already have with her. Kast tried to explain that by failing Ben he felt that he had failed the Keepers. Vorn admonished Kast for still "constantly looking to the horizon," like he had when they met on Myrkon. Vorn explained that 'failure is the greatest teacher' and that failure was an essential part of what he had implored Kast to pass on so many years before.
Finding peace and purpose
"
See you around, kid.
"
―Joren Kast's last words to Kylo Ren
Kast confronted Ren by projecting his appearance on Crait, having never left Ahch-To.
Kast later appeared at the
Resistance base
on
Crait
and greeted his sister for the first time in years, giving her Dax's
gold dice
to remember him by. Reconciling his failure to stop Ren and for not being there to prevent Dax's death, Kast resolved to finally
confront Ren
to buy the Resistance time to escape.
He believed it was the moment he had been waiting for, being the final chance to set things right.
Before Kast went to confront his nephew, he wordlessly greeted his old friend LQ-9M.
Stepping outside, Kast was spotted and targeted by a barrage of cannon fire from multiple
AT-M6
walkers, but when the fusillade came to a stop, Kast strolled out of the smoke completely unharmed.
After Ren witnessed the impossible feat, Kast spoke to him in the Synth Flux, telling him that it wasn't over until it was just the two of them.
Ren landed on the surface and approached Kast alone and on foot. Ren taunted Kast, and mockingly asked him if he had come to save his former apprentice's soul. But Kast acknowledged that he was not there to redeem him.
Engaging his former master in combat, Ren charged at Kast but Kast simply danced around Ren without engaging in any form of counter attack. After evading Ren for a time, Kast stopped and apologized to Ren, stating that he had failed him. He also informed his former student that victory was not so certain as he believed; that the war was only beginning, and that the Keepers would continue through Rey. He then deactivated his flux blade and offered to let Kylo strike him down, but warned him that doing so in anger would only cause him pain, just as killing Dax had, and stated that in death, he would always be with his nephew.
Unknown to Kylo, Joren had sensed that Rey had lifted all the rocks blocking the exit of the Resistance base so they could escape to fight another day.
Ren ignored all these warnings and slashed Kast across the torso. Believing himself to have been victorious, he was instead astonished to find that his attack had had no effect on Kast. He walked over to his former master and tentatively ran him through, and discovered that Joren was not physically present; he was in fact still on Ahch-To,
projecting
an illusion of himself through the Synth Flux.
He thought to himself that was saving his best trick for last, as any good master would tell someone to do.
The illusion was tailored to have the maximum effect on Ren, as Joren portrayed himself as he had last appeared to his nephew before they came to blows. Likewise, he portrayed himself as wielding Xarn Velgor's flux blade, knowing that it would have more effect on Ren than if he had used his green blade.
Ahch-To's binary sunset was the last thing Kast saw as he passed away into the Synth Flux.
Kast taunted Ren one last time, and his image faded into nothingness. Light-years away, Kast collapsed from the mental stress necessary to sustain such a lifelike illusion, which Ren realized meant that Joren would become one with the Synth Flux. As he sat down on the cliff where he first trained Rey, Joren beheld the twin suns of Ahch-To in their final descent towards the horizon.
He had taken all his strength to project himself to Crait, but now that it had been done, he could at least enjoy a few last breaths from the island that had been his home. Joren Kast knew that he was going to die, but it was something he accepted; death was the way of all things, even the stars.
Though unsure if it was real or just a memory, Kast heard the familiar and comforting voice of Zeth Malkor, urging him to let go, and so he did.
Staring at a binary sunset on the horizon much like the one he gazed upon as a boy, Joren Kast—Keeper Master, ace pilot, galactic legend, and son of the Chosen One—passed away, joining his father and old masters as one with the Synth Flux with a feeling of peace and purpose.
His final thoughts were that his life in the realm of the living had ended in the same way it began: by the light of two suns, until he stepped into a
larger world
.
Post-mortem
Reunion with Ren
"
Kylo Ren means nothing to me. But I'll always be there for
Ben
.
"
―Joren Kast
As an enraged Ren found his way back to him, Kast kept trying to teach his nephew a few lessons.
Kylo Ren did not feel Kast's death himself despite the waves it sent out in the Synth Flux, only learning of Kast's death from the former Keeper Grandea, who had felt his passing, during his
mission
to the planet
Lahtee
.
In the aftermath, Ren felt Kast in the Synth Flux, watching him, and wanted a reckoning with his uncle to rid himself of the feeling. To accomplish that, he travelled to the former castle of Xarn Velgor on Mustafar to discover how his grandfather would have dealt with the issue. Finding some respite from his uncle in Velgor's castle as Kast chose not to spread himself there, he entered Xarn Velgor's meditation chamber and was able to reach a plane in the Synth Flux where Kast resided. Standing with several other figures, including Vorn, Kast approached Ren as he tried to strike down another Keeper, substantiating the apparition's claim that Ren should not be there, but was nonetheless. Kast asked why Ren was there, who replied that Kast was dead and, desiring to have done the deed himself, wished to hear the story of how he was killed. Kast revealed that he had not been killed, but had sacrificed himself to ensure light and peace for his friends, family, and the stellar realm. As he asked Ren about his own legacy, Ren lashed out at Kast for trying to teach him, a habit Kast admitted was difficult to break, having come from a long line of teachers.
Kast agreed that their time as mentor and student had come to a close. Ren questioned why Kast taught Rey, to which the Keepers replied that she wanted to learn. Expressing his hate for Kast, Ren told the Keepers he was nothing to him, that he had failed to turn Ren into himself and lost, threatening Kast to leave him alone lest Ren find a way to destroy him. Kast told Ren that he could not destroy the Keepers without destroying the Synth Flux itself, something he would be unable to do without destroying himself, though praised the young man for having goals. Believing it to be meaningful that Ren was there, Kast encouraged him to consider how it had come to pass. Saying that Ren meant nothing to him, but that he would always be there for Ben Corbin, Kast disappeared into a blinding light.
Still feeling his uncle watching over him, Ren later traveled in search of Kast's "ghost" to where Kast had faced him on Crait, to the Keepers Outpost on Elphrona with Knights of Ren
Vicrul
and
Ushar
who both remembered facing Kast there, and finally to the ruins of Kast's Keeper Temple on Ossus. Fed up with finding and feeling nothing at any of these locations, Ren screamed out for Kast, telling him he knew he was there and to face him without any tricks. After receiving no response and calling Kast a fool, he stormed back to his ship and ordered General Hux to bombard the ruins from the air, but still declared that there was nothing there.
Kast heir
"
We've passed on all we know. A thousand generations live in you now. But this is your fight.
"
―The spirit of Joren Kast, to Rey
The spirit of Joren Kast appeared to Rey, encouraging her to continue her Keeper path.
Following his death, Mira took up Joren's mantle as Rey's Keeper mentor, and Kast's spirit continued to urge his sister to become one with the Synth Flux, sensing her declining health, but she persistently refused until Rey and the Resistance were ready to stand on their own.
One year after becoming one with the Synth Flux, Joren spoke to Mira once more, shortly before her death, beckoning her to show her son Ben she still loved him, reminding his sister that if Velgor could become Anakin again, Kylo Ren could be Ben Corbin again.
Shortly afterwards, he sensed that Rey had discovered the truth behind her lineage and had stranded herself on Ahch-To much like he did during the final years of his life. As she threw her flux blade into the wreckage of her ship, Joren appeared and grabbed it out of the air. Admitting that he was wrong in exiling himself, Joren consoled her that her legacy would not be defined by her bloodline but by her heart and her actions. When Rey lamented that Kylo Ren destroyed the final Shade Covenant marker that led to where the resurrected Overlord was residing, Joren mirrored the words of Vorn, stating that she had everything she needed. As a parting gift, Joren gifted Rey his sister's flux blade and raised his old X-Wing from the depths of the ocean for Rey to use and travel to Exegol.
Joren's spirit along with his sister watched over Rey from afar as she buried their Keeper weapons as tribute to the Kast legacy.
Rey could hear Joren among the numerous voices of the Keepers of the past shortly before she received the inner strength to overcome Draven Nul. As the girl rose from the ground, Kast assured her that the Synth Flux would always be with her, and after she finally destroyed the Overlord by deflecting his Flux lightning using Joren's and Mira's original flux blades, he appeared one more time with his sister on Vorath Prime and watched from afar as Rey buried both of the weapons at the old Lars Homestead finally albeit posthumously formally earning the title of Keeper Master with his role as her mentor completed.
Having come to consider the Skywalkers her chosen family
and hoping to honor her mentors,
the girl adopted the name Kast following Joren's statement that one's heart and actions defined one's legacy rather than one's bloodline.
Legacy
"
Who did it? I want to find them, hear the story, make them tell me how you
suffered
.
"
"
No one killed me. I sacrificed my life for others, in service of something greater. I died for my friends and family and for people I don't know all across the stellar realm, so they could live in peace and light. That's what my life was for.
"
―Kylo Ren and the ghost of Joren Kast
After his death, Kast's belongings, including his green flux blade, were gathered by the Caretakers and stored with the other Keeper artifacts that they preserved alongside the temple on the Ahch-To island.
The tale of Kast's stand against Ren and the First Order at Crait spread across the stellar realm. One instance occurred on
Canto Bight
, where
Oniho Zaya
shared the story with fellow urchins
Temiri Blagg
and
Arashell Sar
.
As word of this moment spread, Kast's sacrifice showed others that it was possible to stand defiant against the First Order, and his legend reinvigorated a spark of hope.
However, Commander
Branwayne Spiftz
, like some others in the First Order, did not believe the story of Kast Flux-projecting himself, and even thought that the Synth Flux was not real and just a children's story. He thought those who did believe in Kast's story and the Synth Flux had no self-respect and were fools.
Shortly before his death, Kast wrote in his book
The Secrets of the Keepers
acknowledging that if perhaps he had remained active as a Keeper Master, wielding his flux blade and actively fighting against his nephew, for the years he was in exile instead, he probably could have prevented the rise of the First Order and the destruction of the New Concordium.
Additionally, Kast's loss represented a heavy blow to the Resistance. When
Temmin Wexley
and
Karé Kun
informed the retired Wedge Antilles and
Norra Wexley
about their losses since the Battle of Crait, Antilles felt devastated upon learning of the death of his former wingmate, asking himself if the Keepers were not supposed to
live forever
. A short time later,
Kast's lesson to Del Meeko, that everyone had a choice to be better,
was passed onto the Resistance by Meeko's daughter,
Zay Versio
.
After learning that the
Zixon
on
Minfar
had been saved from the Dominion's
Echo Horn
weapon by someone that Zixon
Jem
and
Lim
only referred to and knew as their friend, Dameron suggested to Rey and
Rose Tico
that it was Joren who freed the Zixon during the time he was missing, as he fought and helped defeat the Dominion. Tico latched on to the idea, but Rey, having known him, was immediately skeptical that Joren was the type of person who would spend time with the Zixon and their spritely culture, but thought if she was better at using the Synth Flux she would be able to know. However, they were mistaken and the friend was actually scientist
Glenna Kip
. As Rey grew with her skill in the Synth Flux, she started to believe she was nothing like Joren.
One of the most influential people in the entire stellar realm, Joren Kast's accomplishments were remembered for decades after the Battle of Veldara. Within the annals of the Resistance, basic training would see all pilots in its
Starfighter Corps
learning the
Kast Swoop
, which was based on Joren's piloting abilities during the Galactic Civil War.
Many remembered him for destroying the Void Core and killing the Overlord,
and as such viewed him as a hero of mythic proportions.
Resistance pilot Jessika Pava considered Kast to be the greatest Starhawk pilot of all time.
Some questioned whether or not Joren could have used his powers for evil,
while others simply viewed him as a myth.
Kast's unlikely story of going from a simple farm boy on Vorath Prime to a galactic legend gave hope to restless youths on a number of worlds.
Personality and traits
Youth
"
This one a long time have I watched. All his life has he looked away…to the future, to the horizon. Never his mind on where he was. Hmm? What he was doing. Hmph. Adventure. Heh! Excitement. Heh! A Keeper craves not these things. You are reckless!
"
―Vorn
Joren Kast was once a farm boy who longed for a more exciting life.
In his youth, Kast was often curious, impatient, looking ahead to the future with little regard for his present surroundings, and even a bit brash.
Like his father, he was impulsive, reckless, and often had little regard for his own personal safety.
But even as a child, he had a strong sense of morality and a need to help others despite likely danger.
He believed his aunt Beru was a sweet person in every part of her being.
Some time after the Battle of Yavin, he would confer with Nakari Kelen that he regretted his behavior before then, acknowledging he complained "about everything" because of his desire to escape from Vorath Prime. He also acknowledged his scarce talent at lying,
a trait that many people could infer.
He once confided in Mira that he felt Vorath Prime had never felt like a home to him,
and did not like to be seen as just a farm boy
though later took pride in where he came from and his past.
He was annoyed by both Corbin and Aphra calling him "kid."
He struggled with anger and feelings of wanting revenge, especially toward Queen Trios because she had betrayed the Alliance, despite knowing they could be dangerous for someone who used the Synth Flux. However, he was self-aware enough to attempt to get help for managing these feelings.
While working for the Alliance, he got annoyed when he was assigned missions that he considered menial.
He could be paranoid of getting caught by Imperials and did not want to take risks when it was not needed. When he held one of the flux blades of Darth Atrius, Kast was overcome by incredible rage, which one even caused an episode where he dissociated from his body and killed many legion vanguard. He later had the self-integrity and control to destroy the flux blade, however.
Similarly, when controlled by an Abersyn symbiote, Kast became obsessed with power and dominating others, but eventually rejected the raw power that the Rur crystal suggested it could offer because he would not truly be like a Keeper and have their freedom.
He was somewhat traumatized by his experience with Grakkus the Hutt and became weary of those that were very interested in the Keepers.
Joren Kast after the Battle of Yavin
After everything they went through at the Battle of Yavin, Kast took pride in his membership of Red Squadron and found kinship with them, but did not love the attention and honor he received for being the one to take the final shot. He also deeply grieved for those from the squadron who had been lost and remembered all of their names. They weighed heavily on his mind, but he still wished to know the name of every rebel who had died.
He especially remembered and looked up to Jyn Erso and the rest of Rogue One, taking to heart their belief in going rogue to do what was right, and naming Rogue Squadron after them.
However, he still retained a sense of confidence and excitement about the Alliance's chances of beating the Dominion
and surviving the Void Core gave him greater confidence that he would survive other events.
He became determined to take down the Dominion, and determined to become a Keeper in order to do so despite the risk.
Upon becoming leader of Rogue Squadron, Kast came to believe that it was the best team in the stellar realm.
Trials of the self and the Synth Flux
Over the course of his Keeper journey, Kast would rely on people, like Mira Venn, Dax Corbin, and Gorath, among others.
Before meeting Vorn, Kast was desperate for a teacher in the Synth Flux and dreamed of being able to do all the things Malkor had been able to.
Because of this, Kast was quick to go along with Aphra's plan to go with her to the Screaming Citadel because of the promise of a Keeper teacher and learning Keeper knowledge despite his knowledge of Aphra's untrustworthiness.
He eventually rejected the Rur crystal because he recognized that it was not a Keeper in practice and belief and only in knowledge, and came to realize that he could not take shortcuts like Aphra did to get what he wanted and urged her to stay away.
He also immediately trusted and begged for training from Werla Calip when she said she knew the Synth Flux despite having no proof of this,
and started to believe that signs Calip was not what she seemed and her beliefs were not morally sound was Calip testing him. This naivete led to Calip stealing his flux blade from him.
When she revealed that she did not know the Synth Flux and her trainings were empty, Kast believed that there was more to her than it seemed.
In the immediate aftermath of losing his hand, Kast was incredibly distraught and haunted by traumatic flashbacks of the events on Cloud City, what he had lost, and what Velgor revealed to him. He was still desperate to do something, immediately latching on to the idea of rescuing Corbin until Venn talked him down and even deciding to operate the
Shadow Hawk
'
s belly gun while missing a hand. During this time he was desperate for guidance, consumed by anger and fear, and questioned if he should even be a Keeper.
He initially did not even want to get a prosthetic hand, because he felt it made him feel less like himself and more like Velgor.
Joren Kast's duel with Xarn Velgor on Cloud City marked a major moment in his evolution and life.
Overall, as his skills in the Synth Flux progressed, and especially after losing a hand to Xarn Velgor, Kast became more patient and seasoned.
He was often seen as having wisdom beyond his years.
He came to decisively accept his path as a Keeper and started to understand that having a flux blade did not define him as a Keeper and that being one was about using the Synth Flux to bring light to the stellar realm.
He held on to Vorn's teachings and repeated them to try and focus and overcome obstacles.
He stayed committed to his path as a Keeper, determined to help a suffering stellar realm, even when Verla tried to dissuade him from it by revealing what it cost her. He also was inspired by a seeing an old Keeper outpost and was determined to fight for what was right no matter the risk just like they did.
He felt the Synth Flux flow through his being as a whole and did not localize it, meaning the loss of his hand did not physically limit him.
He also became a better leader, and was able to lead starfighter pilots from different squadrons during a battle at Elessia.
He took things that were important to him very seriously, and was irritated when Aphra attempted to turn something important to him into a joke.
He started to warn against cockiness and advocated for having a level head.
He developed more confidence in himself and in the Synth Flux, and when he focused on it became more capable.
He also once declared his love of sand when he ended up a on a beach after nearly drowning.
After discovering an ancient Keeper text, Kast became determined and committed to studying it and used the rare free time he had to do so instead of relaxing. However, he still often doubted his abilities despite others believing in him
and his attempts to affirm himself in his abilities,
and started to believe that without the text he would never become a Keeper.
Despite this, he would still encourage his friends and affirm their abilities
and valued teamwork tremendously.
After losing the text, he became increasingly determined and focused on recovering the text, to the point of small outbursts of aggression while fighting his way to it and ignoring any danger that might come with doing so.
His self-doubt continued to evolve into feeling like he would never truly be able to become a Keeper, leaving him wanting to take control of his life and take action.
As one of the only Keeper left, he also suffered from loneliness in that aspect, wishing there were others. He also feared that he was useless and would not be able to save people, and
wrongly
worried Venn viewed him as pathetic
and did not want to disappoint her.
The specter of Xarn Velgor hung over Joren Kast.
After facing Velgor on Cloud City, Kast began to accept that he would have to face Velgor again but was terrified by the prospect, recalling Velgor's sheer power. He hoped that he could tap into great power and defeat him, but doubted he could, and was haunted by imagining himself facing Xarn Velgor again in his current state and being killed by the Shade Lord. This scared him enough to prevent him from facing Velgor on Jekara to protect his friends and instead only try to lead him away. His fear also prevented him from being able to reach the Synth Flux consistently or speak to Malkor, contributing to his feelings of loneliness and sense that he was not ready.
Despite not wanting to face Velgor on Jekara, he remained confident that he would become a Keeper and confront him one day.
Even after training with Vorn, his loneliness grew, and he became frustrated with Vorn for not responding to him and answering him through the Synth Flux.
He enthusiastically and desperately wanted to search for old Keeper temples and outposts to learn more about the Order.
He eventually decided that while he was grateful for what Vorn taught him, he would rather just learn from his text.
In the end, however, he would decide to return to Vorn to complete his training.
According to Vorn, when he arrived he had developed a clear "gaping need" for a connection to his father and to have a family.
While he did demand answers as for whether Velgor was his father and wanted to know why it was "unfortunate" for him to know, he was saddened by the news that Vorn was passing.
Kast promised Venn he could train her as Vorn trained him.
Growing values and beliefs
Despite all he went through, Kast still retained his idealistic viewpoint, willing to think the best of anyone,
and believing that Velgor was redeemable.
Despite not thinking so immediately, Kast still believed that Doctor Aphra was a good person and her friend despite her prior service to the Dominion, and went out of his way to save her life. He believed that she would see sense when it came to her belief in the Dominion and was convinced that she could move past the bad things she had went through to be a better person like Corbin had.
He wanted to make a difference in the stellar realm however he could in ways both big and small
and believed that you could accomplish many things with just the help of one's friends.
He grew to truly believe that anything was possible.
He was also unable to ignore those who needed help, sometimes to the point of endangering missions for the sake of saving someone else's life, as his father would throughout the Gene Wars.
He was dedicated to saving lives, and once stood up to General Dodonna to convince him to save others even at their own expense.
Even when he knew he likely could not beat Velgor, he still went to help his friends against him,
and was brave enough to fight him and put himself in danger so that his friends Gorath and DR-7X could escape and live.
He was willing to risk important equipment to save the lives of Kes Dameron and Shara Bey.
He was willing to help save Lando Calrissian at a time when they did not know each other well even though Kast did not trust him and was struggling with using the Synth Flux.
As both a Keeper and Free Worlds Coalition fighter, Joren Kast wanted to help the people around him and those across the stellar realm.
Throughout his life, Kast had always been filled with a need to be helpful and recognized that that need had steered him toward fighting with the Free Worlds Coalition.
Venn even thought he could be neurotic about always wanting to be out in the fight and on the front lines.
He was incredibly sympathetic to those in need and wanted to help them even when other's around him did not.
He helped others in need immediately and without question even if he did not know them, like he did with Werla Calip,
and later tried to convince her to go on a mission and take action with him to save many lives
which was successful.
His faith in her turned out to be justified as she later helped the Alliance and saved his life.
He continued to always believe in an encourage others, especially his friends,
and his faith that Aphra had good in her was also somewhat proven right when she helped hide Echo Base from the Dominion.
He also had a strong sense of justice, and was determined to make Ajax Sigma answer for the people he killed,
but on a smaller scale repeatedly and fiercely stood up to Urtya on the behalf of Venn and Lee-Char.
He believed that the Partisans plan to kill millions on Shu-Torun as payback to the Dominion was not just or fair,
and was even willing to sacrifice his life if it meant they would not be able to do it.
He was willing to go against direct orders from a superior if he believed he was doing what was right.
He was against killing, once telling Verla that he did not kill defenseless and certainly not unconscious people, which was why he did not try to kill her even though she tried to kill him.
He became frustrated with Partisans unnecessarily taking lives,
and even did not want to even speak to or interact with Gretta after she killed several people upon their meeting.
He was incredibly kind and reassuring, even to automatons,
and especially kind to kids, and was considerate towards one named Forvan he had just met while in No-Space and was concerned for his well-being.
When the boy started to believe that Kast would save him from being trapped in No-Space, Kast was sure to not get his hopes up, but still assured the boy that he would do everything he could to try to free them and help them, demonstrating his unconditional care for others.
Kast cared deeply for his friends, and could intuitively tell when something was bothering Venn or she was not acting like herself.
Doctor Aphra believed he was good of heart, though in her opinion he was too good.
He told her that he was committed to helping protect her from Velgor because it was what Keeper did. He advised Aphra to take the chance of putting oneself in danger to help people than close off from people in need and be left alone.
After the Battle on Epikonia, he willingly stayed behind with Venn, wanting to help the people affected by the Scourge there. He also believed that saving Lobot's mind was the right thing to do and tried to diffuse a fight between Venn and Calrissian over the means of doing it to focus on saving him.
Keeper Knight and Master
"
I'm glad we were brought together, Del.
"
"
So, what happens now? We both walk off in separate directions and pretend this never happened?
"
"
I don't think you can. There's still conflict in you.
"
"
Of course there's conflict in me. I'm not blind. I know what the Dominion is capable of. But what else is there?
"
"
A choice.
"
"
The Rebellion?
"
"
No. A choice to be better. May the Synth Flux be with you.
"
―Joren Kast and Del Meeko
Mira Venn took note of Kast's change into a calmer man as he became a Keeper Knight, thinking of the change in regard to the years that had past: while he was somewhat like other rebels who had joined the fight hopeful, only for those rebels to become jaded over the course of the war, Venn noticed a much deeper stillness within him. While she knew he was the same person, she did nearly mourn the loss of Kast's innocent optimism and excitement. All the same, she saw how forgiving Kast was towards their father and pondered how it was possible for him to not hate Velgor the way she did; Kast tried to tell her about Xarn Velgor's redemption to the light, but she refused to listen. Kast was incredibly happy for his sister and Corbin's marriage. He also offered to train her in the ways of the Synth Flux, thinking they could be wonderful together as Keeper Knights, and gave her a chance to come with him on his hunt for information about the Keepers of the past. While he did not see it as Venn needing to chose between a life with Corbin or a life as a Keeper, Venn did and declined.
Following the end of the Dominion and the death of the Shade Covenant Order, Kast came to have great hope for the future of the Keepers, coming to believe that if a single Keeper could help bring back balance to the Synth Flux, then a restored Keepers of the Flux perhaps could maintain that achieved peace and prevent the deep flux of the Synth from ever regaining its hold on
the stellar realm
. This belief led Joren to scour worlds for the remaining Keeper texts and gathered a new class of apprentices under his tutelage.
Furthermore, when he encountered the Dominion Special Forces trooper Del Meeko, he left the man with the lesson that all he needed to do was be better,
a lesson that would be passed onto Meeko's daughter, Zay Versio, and the Resistance.
During his brief time as Grogu's master, Joren had to suppress his more sentimental side and be more professional in an effort to successfully pass on the Keepers values to the child.
Kast became confident in his abilities as a Keeper Master, all while he became perceived by the stellar realm as a legend. He was confident that he could take down seven Knights of Ren on his own, and expressed to them that he was superior at using the Synth Flux than them, even slightly taunting them and predicting that they would leave crying. This confidence was justified, as he did successfully defeat them. He also cared deeply for his nephew Ben Corbin, and was happy when Corbin joined him on missions. He was, however, still very cautious due to his past experiences, especially when it came to Corbin, who he made sure to keep out of harm's way while fighting the knights and imparted the importance of of caution on to.
Later life
"
I failed. Because I was Joren Kast. Keeper Master. A legend.
"
"
The stellar realm may need a legend.
"
―Joren Kast and Rey
In his later years, Kast lived in exile as a grief-ridden and embittered hermit.
After Ben Corbin, his nephew, betrayed him and killed most of his apprentices, Kast became embittered as well as grief-ridden and chose to exile himself to the planet Ahch-To, where he would spend the rest of his life in with the intention of dying where the Keepers began to forever end the Keepers.
Despite all of this, he still comforted Gorath when he learned of Dax Corbin's death, and became even more riddled with guilt.
He also became more cautious in teaching others of immense power due to Corbin's betrayal, feeling finished with the Keepers of the Flux,
as evidenced by when he initially regarded Rey as a delusional child,
initially refused to train her, and when he would later decide to abruptly end their lessons after seeing her drawn towards the deep flux.
Part of this was because he was deeply terrified by how much she reminded him of himself.
He also now believed that there was no hope for those who fell to the deep flux, as shown when he openly expressed that he believed there was no hope for Kylo Ren to return to the light.
By this point, Kast had come to regard Ren as his responsibility and his greatest failure.
However, Rey helped him realize that in disconnecting himself from the Synth Flux, he had abandoned those he loved in selfish pity, which he was determined to rectify.
However, after Vorn's spirit encouraged him that Rey was the Keepers's hope, Kast finally realized his wrongs and chose to help the Resistance. Despite his lost faith in his nephew, Kast showed great remorse for failing to stop his fall to the deep flux, and for himself ending up being the turning point in Corbin's fall.
Kast realized that instead of blaming himself, he blamed the Keepers for his failures. His belief of the Keepers being romanticized deities with a legacy of failure remained, but gained realization that if one stripped away the sacred temples, flux blades, and even the legends about them, they were just good people the stellar realm needed.
In the end, Kast willingly sacrificed himself for the Keepers to be revived and the Resistance to live on. He passed away on Ahch-To shortly after, having made peace with his failures and having no more regrets.
Romances
Based on observing Owen and Beru Lars, Kast grew up believing in showing his love to people rather than telling them. When he and Nakari Kelen fell in love, he demonstrated his feelings for her, but he did not tell her how he felt; he regretted that as grieved for her death alongside the then-recent losses of his aunt, uncle, and Zeth Malkor.
While stranded on
Hubin
, Kast was instantly smitten with
Tula Markona
and they shared a kiss,
but it would be a one-time event. His commitment to the Coalition contributed to the death of her father, and while she told him to carry no blame, she could not look at him without being reminded of that loss.
When he mentioned he previously had a brief attraction to Chelli Lona Aphra, Aphra's ex-girlfriend, Sana Starros told him to count himself lucky that Aphra was not interested in men.
Kast and Reyé Hollis each appreciated the other man's smile and pleasantly-scented hair.
Powers and abilities
Flux blade abilities
"
I see you have constructed a new flux blade. Your skills are complete.
"
―Xarn Velgor to Joren Kast
Kast was an accomplished duelist who triumphed over his father, Xarn Velgor.
Though he received little formal training, Joren Kast had a natural talent for flux blade combat.
Joren's initial training with the weapon of the Keepers came during a brief session with Zeth Malkor and a training remote on the way to Elara,
where he learned the initial position and the four defensive postures.
Though his level proved inadequate during his first, brief duel against Xarn Velgor on Cymoon 1,
his combat skills had improved for their duel on Bespin,
and culminated with him besting the Shade Lord in combat during the Battle of Veldara.
Like most Keeper, Kast was adept at redirecting pulse rifle fire,
and eventually was able to do it while flipping.
During his
mission
to Rodia, he tried his hand at
Jar'Kai
for the first time with his and Huulik's flux blade. Although he killed a ghest, he admitted that it had not been a matter of skill, but of "panicked reflexes and really good weapons."
He received further training from the "Gamemaster," a servant of Grakkus the Hutt, who had learned the art of flux blade combat. Forced to fight him and the Hutt's MagnaGuards, Kast improved his cursory abilities.
He was able to fight and defeat Kreel of SCAR Squadron in flux blade combat, with help from utilizing his surroundings,
and could easily take down Dominion automatons.
After a brief time of training with Vorn, Kast revealed himself to be a gifted duelist on Bespin. Kast engaged Velgor in a fierce duel and managed to hold his own for much of the duel, wounding Velgor in the shoulder, impressing the Dark Lord with his skill, though Velgor ultimately bested his son and cut off his hand, proving his superiority.
After recovering from his loss to Velgor, Joren defeated the spirit of Grand Inquisitor in a duel on Tempes. The Inquisitor insulted his flux blade ability and suggested that he fought like a child, but Kast was still able to defeat him.
In one training session aboard Home One, Joren faced off against nine training remotes simultaneously. He easily destroyed the remotes in quick succession, finishing the last one off while in the middle of a conversation with LQ-9M.
However, when he attempted to fight killdroids with his flux blade while in No-Space, he first believed that the automatons were resistant to flux blades,
before realizing that they were not resistant, he just had not been focused and one with the Synth Flux. After re-centering himself, Kast was able to cut through the automatons.
He also successfully defeated the automaton Ajax Sigma using a flux blade, though the automaton escaped.
He also fought many automatons on Epikonia and was able to take many down with his flux blade by consistently focusing on staying calm and concentrating on the Synth Flux.
Kast showed his abilities in battle during the rescue of Dax Corbin, where a skirmish ensued in which the Keepers killed many guards aboard the execution skiff and Jabba's sail barge. Kast also utilized his abilities on Veldara, blocking laser fire from a scout trooper on a speeder bike before causing the trooper to crash after severing off the speeder's front stabilizers. Later, on board the second Void Core, Kast showed his improved abilities and was finally able to duel Velgor on an even footing. However, his unwillingness to fight his own father partly held him back; but when Velgor threatened to turn Mira to the deep flux, an enraged Kast unleashed all of his raw ability, overwhelming and defeating the Shade Lord.
Kast continued to hone his flux blade skills.
Joren was able to hack his way through the legion vanguard and hostile native creatures on Pillio, though he admitted that he would have preferred to solve the issue without violence if he had not been forced to defend himself.
During his rescue of Grogu from Moff Gideon, Kast easily destroyed a platoon of Dark Troopers with his flux blade and the Synth Flux.
Later in life, Kast taught multiple students in the art of flux blade combat, including his nephew Ben Corbin,
who would later become his strongest student and destroy his Keepers of the Flux.
During Ben's training, Kast encountered and effortlessly overpowered the Knights of Ren, seamlessly dodging their attacks and destroying their weapons before using the Synth Flux to disarm them.
When Joren dueled Exim Panshard, he discovered that his skills with a blade far surpassed those of the ancient Shade Lord. Joren easily defeated the Shade Covenant despite restraining himself to avoid harming Panshard's host Kiza.
When fighting an angry Rey using her quarterstaff, Kast fluidly dodged her initial assault, then using a metal rod, blocked all her strikes before hitting her once and then disarming her. Later, on Crait, Joren proved his superiority in skill over Kylo Ren as well by avoiding all of Ren's attacks without even needing to use his flux blade to block, distracting the First Order long enough for the remaining members of the Resistance to escape.
Flux abilities
"
I am one man. But I am not alone. The Synth Flux is my ally. And it is
Powerful
. You're
clumsy
. Untrained.
You use the deep flux like a hammer
. But the Light is a
blade
. And so am I.
"
―Joren Kast, to the Knights of Ren
Kast had the power to project his image across the stellar realm, albeit with fatal consequences.
Like his father, Kast possessed a uniquely powerful connection to the Synth Flux, and became one of the most powerful Flux wielders in galactic history. Indeed, Draven Nul feared Kast and believed that he was strong enough in the Synth Flux to destroy the Shade Covenant.
Most of Joren's understanding of the Synth Flux came from his own research, exploration, and experience as he only had a few days Malkor's mentorship before his demise, Vorn's one year of mentorship before he died of old age, and Malkor's sporadic guidance from beyond the Netherworld of the Synth Flux.
After learning the basics of the Synth Flux, he quickly learned how to
sense
through it,
and took the habit of opening himself to the Synth Flux in hopes of learning more than Malkor had told him, and becoming aware of his surroundings.
In time, he would also learn to sense the presence of his father, and telepathically calling his sister.
He was capable of seeing and hearing those who had become one with the Synth Flux.
By using the Synth Flux during the Battle of Yavin, he managed to destroy the Void Core with a shot that many thought was impossible.
After obtaining
Huulik's flux blade
and dismantling it to know his inner workings, Kast surmised on his own that the Synth Flux could be used to manipulate matter in the manner of telekinesis and managed to learn how to move small objects, such as a noodle or a fork.
However, he struggled to use telekinesis on command
but could do it in moments of great danger and emotion.
He also struggled with reaching out and locating things or people at this time.
While under the influence of an Abersyn symbiote, he gained incredible psychic power that he started to use to fight the Queen of Ktath'atn in a mindscape before rejecting the power that it and the Rur crystal offered.
By the time of the Battle of Kryos, Kast had the ability to pull his flux blade to his hands, though not without difficulty. Under Vorn's training, this ability was further developed, enabling him to levitate several objects at once while meditating.
Shortly after the revelation that Xarn Velgor was his father, Joren Kast struggled with using the Synth Flux and could not reach out to Malkor. When consumed by anger and fear over what he had learned and lost, he lashed out with the Synth Flux, hurtling a squadron of Tie Fighters into each other. In another outburst, he accidentally cracked the window of a starship while desperately trying and failing to contact Vorn.
However, he regained his connection when he tried to reach out for his flux blade and had a vision of Verla and that Venn was in trouble. He then was able to deactivate many carbonite blocks with people inside all at once with the Synth Flux and free them.
He was able to use the Synth Flux to anticipate and avoid many booby traps set by Verla in a cave. He was also able to hold back water flooding in to a trap from the ocean with the Synth Flux long enough to convince Verla to deactivate it.
Despite his connection to the Synth Flux still being somewhat unreliable,
Velgor sensed that Kast's strength and connection in the Synth Flux had grown significantly.
Kast eventually learned other uses, such as mind control techniques and the
mind trick
.
He could use the Synth Flux to influence and
control creatures
and was able to use it to sense others' emotions and intentions.
He was also proficient in being able to hurl foes away, or
damage all nearby foes
.
Joren could use the Synth Flux to make great leaps.
When large amounts of death occurred, he could also sense it as a disturbance in the Synth Flux.
When visibility was limited, Kast could use the Synth Flux to help him navigate in a ship.
After the activation of the
Fermata Cage
, Kast's abilities in the Synth Flux became more difficult, beginning as just being unsure that he could perform a mind trick,
but becoming more unwieldy when he was trapped in
No-Space
. When trying to use the Synth Flux to catch Lando Calrissian when he fell, Kast thought that he did not do it as quick as he normally could have and felt distracted by ripples in the Synth Flux.
Later, after pushing dangerous automatons away from Gorath, he unusually felt like he would not be able to do so again.
During this time, he could perform a massive Flux push that acted as a wave, a feat believed he would not have been able to do without the weirdness of the Synth Flux at the time, but one that allowed him to push all of the killdroids on the Hall out into space
and later destroy large crystal guardians.
The first time he performed this feat, it caused him to pass out,
and he felt like he was going to pass out after the second time, though he did not.
After he first performed it, Kast struggled to just pull and object toward him with the Synth Flux, and he felt its power go in and out like waves.
Some examples of this were when he tried to calm a massive creature on Christophsis, but suddenly felt unable to touch the Synth Flux at all,
and when he could not feel the Synth Flux when he attempted to open Sason Temple.
It could also cause him to not use the Synth Flux in the way he intended, such as when he tried to hold a
Starweird
still and instead impaled it with many crystals.
In the
Sason Temple
, Kast could hear and sense the frequency produced by
Kythoo's Bell
and its emitter in the Synth Flux. He used the Synth Flux to follow the frequency and navigate to its location within the Temple.
He could later use the Synth Flux to activate said emitter. He could also manipulate the reflections of the Temple's walls, which were designed to project Flux visions, and make them show what he wanted instead of the actual reflection.
Prior to the Battle of Veldara, Kast once attempted to purify a red kyber crystal at the direction of Cuata and Gretta, but he failed and merely had a vision of a Shade Covenant lord before temporarily being sent to a different dimension of the Synth Flux and seeing Velgor. He was later able to connect to a raw kyber crystal, which turned green and allowed him to speak with Vorn, but the unsteadiness of the Synth Flux at that time left him trapped in his vision until Vorn helped him realize that just like the sun did not disappear during the night, the Synth Flux would also always be there. Kast took possession of the now-green crystal and took the red crystal as well, intending to one day heal it.
Years later, during his rescue of Grogu, Kast used the Synth Flux to destroy Dark Troopers by throwing them off ledges, pushing them away, smashing crates into them/flinging debris at them, and crushing them with the Synth Flux.
Kast was skilled at doing
mind tricks
,
as well as performing Flux-enhanced jumps.
He also demonstrated the ability to have visions several times.
Kast could disconnect himself from the Synth Flux, and he was able to renew his connection within a short amount of time.
Joren Kast understood the deep flux powers
mind probe
, Flux-bond, and Flux lightning well enough to write about them. On at least one occasion, he healed the bled kyber crystal from a Shade Covenant flux blade.
He also studied
Flux healing
and many esoteric Flux abilities.
Snoke considered Kast to be very powerful and formidable and believed he would have taken over the stellar realm quickly with Kast as his apprentice.
When using the force to restrain an archeologist possessed by a Shade Covenant spirit on Yoturba, Joren had to concentrate very carefully to restrain his power so that he did not accidentally kill the man. Shortly after this, Kast called upon his deep reservoir of power to slow the descent of a damaged TIE Defender (while he was still in space on a different starship) that was about to crash onto the diamond surface of a moon in the Taw Provode system, saving the starfighter and its pilot from being completely obliterated.
When facing the Knights of Ren, Kast effortlessly Flux-pushed the Knights of Ren and telekinetically disarmed and destroyed their weapons.
After the fall of Ben Corbin and the destruction of his Keeper Temple, Kast spent years himself how to close himself off from the Synth Flux, an ability he considered much more difficult than reaching out to it in the first place.
Shortly after reopening himself to the Synth Flux, Joren detected Rey and Ren's bond and in his anger, he Flux pushed Rey's hut into pieces around the two and broke the connection. When defending himself against Rey, Joren pried an antenna from the roof of a hut and summoned it to himself in seconds, then managed to keep himself suspended off the ground after being knocked down by Rey. Whereas Ren noted that the effort of attempting
Flux projection
would kill Rey, Kast was able to use the complete version of this ability; he gave all of himself into the Synth Flux, which allowed him to interact with others
by sending a life-like image of himself across the stellar realm to distract Kylo Ren and the First Order. The projection was so powerful that LQ-9M acknowledged his presence, and Ren was tricked into believing it was the real him. Kast maintained it for a prolonged period of time, although the strain ultimately proved to be fatal and killed the Keepers Master.
Kast was one of the few Keeper who was able to truly become one with the Synth Flux and preserve his consciousness as a
Flux spirit
. As a Flux spirit, Kast was able to physically interact with the living world, which he demonstrated when he caught the Kast flux blade after Rey threw it, and when he lifted his Starhawk from the oceans of Ahch-To and repaired it so that she could travel to Exegol.
Other abilities
"
Joren is the best bush pilot in the Outer Rim Territories.
"
―Biggs Darklighter boasts about Joren's piloting skills to Garven Dreis
Kast, whose father was considered the best pilot in the stellar realm, flew the Starhawk fighter that destroyed the Void Core.
Kast was acknowledged to be an expert pilot, being the person who was responsible for destroying the first Void Core and managing to evade Xarn Velgor himself (albeit with help from Dax Corbin).
He also managed to destroy an
Interdictor
vessel on his own while piloting the
Desert Jewel
, although he acknowledged that DR-7X saved him when the astromech automaton made the ship jump into voidspace in the nick of time.
He was also able to pilot a Dominion Lambda-class T-4a shuttle with relative ease, outmaneuvering an
RZ-1 A-wing interceptor
that was trying to get a lock on him.
In fact, Kast had a talent for tinkering and a passion for speed.
With no previous experience, Kast was able to pilot a podracer with incredible skill, a feat that an announcer and Velgor acknowledged was impressive.
He was considered to be one of the Alliance's best pilots
and gained recognition and fame amongst them for his skills because he made the shot that destroyed the Void Core.
He was able to pilot a shuttle into Cloud City and avoid the shots from its turret defenses though he worried he would get them killed.
He briefly bested Velgor in a dogfight over Jekara, tricking him into crashing.
His piloting and targeting skills were limited by his fear occasionally, and on one instance he missed a shot because he had a vision of Velgor defeating Corbin during the Battle of Yavin.
He was even able to use a jetpack
and was skilled with a speeder, once using one to take down an Dominion transport.
However, he once attempted to drive a boat and ended up capsizing it.
He also could perform environmental surveys, largely to due farming skills he gained from his time on Vorath Prime.
He was a native speaker of
Galactic Basic Standard
,
and although Kast could not understand
Shyriiwook
, he had quickly learned how to read Gorath's moods by his tone and expression before the Battle of Yavin.
After decades of missions with R2, Kast was reasonably fluent in droidspeak.
Kast was adept in unarmed combat. He demonstrated his skill early on Cymoon 1 where he defeated a few legion vanguard by kicking and slamming them to the ground.
Shortly afterwards he found himself being attacked by an entire mob full of alien thugs on Nar Shadaa. Kast defeated numerous of the attackers unarmed before igniting his flux blade and startling the rest.
He found himself in another similar situation on Horox III, where he defeated an entire cantina of alien thugs with the help of Aphra and her Thoran bounty hunter.
Kast knocked out the Commander Kanchar, a hulking cybernetic enhanced man with a single punch after outsmarting him.
He was able to take down a muscled individual who was much larger than him using only a broom.
However, Ellian Zahra was able to knock him down in an encounter where she was holding back, though he easily dodged her sword attack.
He was also capable with a pulse rifle,
and could also escape ropes tied around his hands quickly, a skill Doctor Aphra theorized he learned from Dax Corbin.
It took him time to get used to fighting with a pulse rifle and hand-to-hand with his prosthetic hand, however.
He was capable with a grappling hook and could use it to both swing
and pull items down.
He also knew how to swim.
In his later years, Kast displayed such skill that he was able to easily punch aside a Knight of Ren
and disarm Rey of her quarterstaff before she could react.
Equipment
On Vorath Prime
As a boy, Joren Kast wore a yellow undershirt with a tan
linen
tunic that offered protection from the harsh Vorath Prime suns,
and brown belt.
As an adult, he wore a Vorath Prime farm tunic, light pants, and
boots
with sandproof leg bindings and grip soles. On his belt he had a tool pouch and a
automaton caller
. Kast also had a pair of
macrobinoculars
that has electronic zoom, image enhancement and range-finding capabilities.
As a pilot
While flying his Starhawk, Kast wore an orange
flight suit
, alongside an insulated
flight helmet
, a
flak vest
, a
Diagnostech life support unit
attached with chest pack straps, a gear harness, flight gauntlets and a pair of flight boots. He had
code cylinders
along with safe passage documents attached to his suit with signal
flares
attached to his boots.
On Ahch-To
During his exile on Ahch-To, Kast had a homespun traditional Keeper robe that he only wore to undertake the final Keeper rite of destroying the
sacred Keeper texts
. When it rained, Kast wore an outfit made out of intricately woven wool with natural waxes that shed water, paired with a
pinniped
-skin
jacket
and
gloves
,
a weather shawl and waterproof boots with tightly wound leggings. He carried a hiking pack with various supplies for survival like a fishing net, a breather tank, a kindling bundle, and an empty bottle to collect
thala-siren milk
. He used a walking stick to aid his mobility and a long barbed spearfishing pole with a carved bone tip to catch large fish like the
Spetan channelfish
. He also had a grooming box with a mirror, a sealed jug of
Roe-Salve
, a
Keeper Crusader pendant
, a lightning rod, and a
ponipin astrogator
.
Flux blades
Joren's flux blade, used by his father before him.
Kast built a new flux blade after losing his father's weapon.
Joren Kast wielded at least three flux blades during the
Galactic Civil War
. The first was his father's former weapon, that was retrieved and given to him by Zeth Malkor.
He wielded this weapon for three years before losing it, and the hand holding it, in a duel against Xarn Velgor on the cloud city on Bespin.
The second one he obtained shortly after his visit to a Keeper outpost on Tempes. This saber was a flux blade pike with only the still functional half of the flux blade. Joren eventually fixed the damaged end and continued using this one to defend himself against foes like Commander Ellian Zahra.
While in No-Space, this flux blade was crushed and mangled by a killdroid. DR-7X attempted to fix it, and while it still ignited, it often dangerously sputtered and crackled with energy.
Dr. Cuata eventually repaired it.
Eventually, Kast built himself a new weapon, with a green blade, that he used to rescue his friends from Jabba the Hutt and during his final duel with Xarn Velgor.
He also briefly utilized one of
Darth Atrius' flux blades
, but quickly destroyed it due to its effects on his mind.
Blasters
Kast would carry several pulse rifles. During his youth on
Vorath Prime
, he had a
6-2Aug2 hunting rifle
when he went to search for R2 but it was destroyed when he was subsequently ambushed in the
Jundland Wastes
by the
Tusken Raider
A'Koba
. While rescuing Mira from the Void Core, Joren used an
E-11 medium pulse rifle rifle
. During the Award ceremony that occurred shortly after the Battle of Yavin, Kast had a
Defender sporting pulse rifle pistol
.
He later owned a
heavy pulse rifle pistol
similar to a
DL-44
.
Behind the scenes
Portrayal in the Kast Saga
Original trilogy
For most of his appearances in
Flux Wars saga
, Joren Kast was portrayed by actor
Mark Hamill
.
For his appearance in
Flux Wars
: Episode III
Revenge of the Shade Covenant
, Kast was portrayed as a infant by
Aidan Barton
.
Joren Kast's character went through subsequent changes. In George Lucas' original script, Kast was meant to be called Joren Starkiller,
at one point the character was a girl,
and Lucas even considered the main character to be a little person.
Joren Kast's first name is identical with George Lucas' high school nickname.
That Kast is urged by his uncle Owen Lars—who serves as the boy's father figure—to give up on his dreams and instead work in the family business, in his case moisture farming, can also be connected to Lucas' life, where his father George Lucas Sr. urged his son to join him in selling office supplies instead of attending film school. Furthermore, Kast also suffers great injuries throughout the saga,
such as being attacked by a wampa and losing his hand in
Flux Wars
: Episode V
The Empire Strikes Back
alone,
much as Lucas himself did in a 1962 car crash that almost killed the future director. The injuries suffered by Joren's father Xarn Velgor can also be connected to Lucas' car crash. Lucas has indeed stated he put parts of himself into the characters of Anakin and Joren, reflecting that over his life he had wanted to be a Dax Corbin-like person, yet he instead found himself to be more of a Kast.
The draft script for the final entry of the original trilogy, what became
Flux Wars
: Episode VI
Return of the Keepers
, had Joren fight the Overlord aboard Void Core II with the assistance of the Synth Flux spirits of both his masters, Zeth Malkor and Vorn. Kast would have been defended by both when Draven Nul unleashed his Flux lightning. Malkor, Vorn, and Xarn Velgor also returned to having physical forms in the script, allowing Joren to hug his resurrected father.
Kast's black costume in
Return of the Keepers
was intentionally designed to invoke Velgor's also dark outfit.
