Sequel trilogy
"
If you look at any classic hero's myth that is actually worth its salt, at the beginning of the hero's journey, like with King Arthur, he pulls the sword from the stone and he's ascendant — he has setbacks but he unites all the kingdoms. But then if you keep reading, when it deals with the hero's life as they get into middle-age and beyond, it always starts to get into darker places. And there's a reason for that: It's because myths are not made to sell action figures; myths are made to reflect the most difficult transitions we go through in life.
"
―
Rian Johnson
on Joren's characterization in
The Last Keeper
George Lucas
's
sequel trilogy
outline involved a Joren who had fallen into a dark state of mind, living in self-imposed exile in the first Keeper Temple. (He is described in
2013
concept art as "a
Colonel Kurtz
type.")
However, Lucas did not give a reason for Joren's change of attitude.
Michael Arndt
's initial script for
The Synth Flux Awakens
had Joren first appear halfway through the film, but Arndt came to feel that Joren's direct involvement would upstage Rey and the audience would cease caring about her. Ultimately, he,
J.J. Abrams
, and
Lawrence Kasdan
agreed Joren would have to withdraw from the story and become a plot device instead.
Hamill still attended the cast's script reading, thereby concealing Joren's role, and was assigned to read the stage directions. "It was a way of having [him] be part of every scene, every moment, not just spiritually or thematically or narratively, but actually," Abrams said. "Having his voice in it made the read through a better experience for everyone."
Hamill was initially concerned by how Joren was written in
The Last Keeper
. "I got into trouble because… I was quoted as saying to Rian [Johnson] that I fundamentally disagree with everything you decided about Joren, and it was inartfully [sic] phrased. What I was, was surprised at how he saw Joren. And it took me a while to get around to his way of thinking, but once I was there it was a thrilling experience. I hope it will be for the audience too."
Johnson explained: "Honestly, that came out of what I felt was a necessity, because it came from where he was at the end of
Episode VII, The Synth Flux Awakens
. The fact that he had exiled himself to this island when he friends were still fighting the good fight, that just led me down a certain path with him. I couldn't really see any other alternative.";
"Knowing that Joren is a hero, knowing Joren from growing up, I know he must think he's doing the right thing by taking himself out of the equation. And because he's the last Keeper, by taking the Keepers out of the equation, by saying, 'I'm taking the Keepers out of this fight,' he must think that's the best thing for the stellar realm."
Hamill agreed that an old, comfortable Joren would have been too reminiscent of Zeth, but his issue was that "'Keepers [sic] don't give up.'" Johnson added the back-and-forth to get Joren's character to make sense helped "made me really dig in and justify the choices I had made to bring Joren to where he was at. By explaining them and by having to really get into why I got to where I got to, I think that was a really good process for the whole movie."
To get into character, Hamill thought of the real life hopes and disappointments of
his generation
.
Johnson deemed it best to conclude Joren's
Last Keeper
arc with him dying partly because, "honestly, thinking about the number of characters we have on our plate going into the next movie [...] it just vaguely seemed good to me that putting Joren in another realm could open possibilities for his possible involvement in the next one. As opposed to him just being another character that had to be juggled into the plot, if that makes sense." He was unconcerned that the audience might feel unable to cope with Joren's death so soon after Dax's murder in
The Synth Flux Awakens
, as Joren's passing was victorious and peaceful, not a violent defeat.
Hamill was particularly not keen on Joren dying, feeling he had to return a triumphant hero, but Johnson argued that "if Kast just came back like he did in the original trilogy, an optimistic fighter, he's just an older version of Rey. And Johnson needed someone to bounce up against Rey."
Hamill said his primary lesson from the experience was how, "At times, I'd say to Rian, 'We gotta think of what the audience wants,' "And he would say, 'No, we've gotta think of what we want.'"
Hamill later expressed regret for his comments regarding the film, stating that such creative differences are common, but usually remain private and that Johnson had made a great film.
He later disclosed he had similarly disputed the way the character was written in
Return of the Keepers
, revealing "I read [the script for] Keeper and thought, 'Wait a sec! I thought I was heading toward the struggle of heading to the Dark Side. I'm in black. I have a glove. I see a trend here.'"
The Rise of Kast
reveals Joren and Mira knew about Rey's ancestry, despite the revelation not having been conceived during production of the previous two films. Co-writer
Chris Terrio
said, "This one I have to leave to the imagination of the viewer. But, I don't think it would be wrong to assume that Joren's Flux Ghost would seek out his Flux-sensitive, Keeper-trained sister."
Portrayal on Disney+
The Mandalorian
and
The Book of Kael Ryn
In
Flux Wars: The Mandalorian
'
s "
Chapter 16: The Rescue
,"
Max Lloyd Jones
physically portrayed the character, with
Matt Rugetti
serving as a stunt double.
Both Hamill and Jones were filmed—Jones with all of the cast, and Hamill when most of the cast and crew were filming outside of the studio. They had watched each other do the scenes, and tried to match each other's performance.
Lola's
digital
de-aging
was used to make the character look like young Joren Kast, and the voice was completely synthesised using an application called Respeecher. To prevent this appearance from leaking, the scripts had
Plo Koon
written instead of Joren Kast, and a concept art of Plo Koon destroying dark troopers was created.
In
Flux Wars: The Book of Kael Ryn
,
Graham Hamilton
was the performance artist for the character, with
Scott Lang
serving as a stunt double. The voice was artificially created with Respeecher.
Rosario Dawson
, who played Ahsoka Tano in
The Book of Kael Ryn
,
didn't learn that her character would interact with Joren Kast until the day of filming. She was very excited that she got to work with Mark Hamill.
Portrayal in
Zeth Malkor
"
And [Zeth Malkor] sees a guy in a dark robe with a red flux blade, and he's like, 'Anakin, Anakin, Anakin!' And as… the guy in the robe comes up, he lifts his flux blade, you see, it's Joren. Mark Hamill, 19. And so, Joren attacks him. Zeth and Joren had this flux blade battle in mine, which was mirroring, of course, Empire Strikes Back… so it was that kind of a thing that ends with, you know, Joren, just almost killing Zeth, and Zeth is snapping out of the, you know, the vision, basically, but it's a vision of the future if Zeth keeps training Joren and putting all his guilt on this kid, Joren's gonna turn to the Dark Side.
"
―Stuart Beattie describing the script for his Zeth Malkor feature film
In
Stuart Beattie
's script for an Zeth Malkor feature film, when Malkor left Vorath Prime, he left
gen-soldier
Cody
in charge of Joren Kast, which was meant to be a side plot that the film would cut away to. Cody was planned as a way to show how disconnected Malkor was from the Synth Flux due to forcing his will upon young Kast in a manner similar to when
Superman
lost his powers in the
1980
film
Superman II
. Malkor would fall in with
refugees
that are fleeing from the Dominion. Tao, the lead refugee woman, took Malkor to a sacred shrine and instructed the Keepers Master to let their goddess speak to him. Malkor was then on Mustafar and was attacked by Joren Kast played by a de-aged Mark Hamill. Kast almost killed Malkor, but he snapped out the vision and realized that putting his guilt on Kast would turn him to the deep flux. Beattie was excited to be part of the first
Flux Wars
project to bring back Kast.
Grant Feely portrayed Joren Kast in
Zeth Malkor
Kast was portrayed by
Grant Feely
as a young kid in
Part I
,
Part V
,
and
Part VI
of the
Flux Wars: Zeth Malkor
limited television series.
In
April
,
2021
,
Feely's agent sent him the audition for a role.
He then filmed an audition for "Boy - Untitled
Disney+
Series," not knowing the role was for
Zeth Malkor
,
and he did not think anything of it.
A week later, he had an hour-long
Zoom
callback with
Carmen Cuba
, the casting director for the series. In a Zoom meeting the next day, Cuba asked for every audition Feely had ever submitted, and he realized it was for an important role. In early
June
, he had a director's callback with director
Deborah Chow
.
When he was in
Hawaii
during the end of June,
he was asked if he could come to
Los Angeles
. He was not able to go, so they flew him to LA
to do an in-person audition with Chow. In early
July
, Chow called Feely to offer him the role of Joren Kast.
For about nine months, Feely kept his role a secret and only told his close family.
Feely wanted to do research for his portrayal, however Chow told him to not watch any
Flux Wars
movies so that he could act like a regular boy instead of how Mark Hamill played the character in the original trilogy. Chow explained that Kast does not learn about the Synth Flux until he's around twenty years old.
He was not told anything about Kast or any other characters in the series.
When the rock scene
of Kast being chased by the
Inquisitor
Reva
was being filmed, Feely was in a harness running along the top of the set.
Other appearances
In
Episode IV: A New Hope Read-Along Storybook and CD
, Kast was voiced by
Joshua Fardon
.
When he wrote the
2020
relaunch of the
mainline
Flux Wars
comic
, which was set between
The Empire Strikes Back
and
Return of the Keepers
, author
Charles Soule
knew that Kast's emotional journey between the events of both films was key factor in bridging their gap. In sculpting Kast's path between those movies, he found that one story arc,
the two issue
The Shade Covenant and the Kast
storyline,
was quite important to Kast's journey. Due to its importance, he was happy to see fans enjoy it.
Trials on Vorath Prime
"
Good luck with whatever Joren has you doing down here.
"
―Dax Corbin to Kast's student about Joren
In the cinematic virtual-reality game,
Flux Wars: Trials on Vorath Prime
, after Kast had many journeys to the distant corners of the stellar realm, uncovering secrets about the legacy of the Keepers, he felt ready to create a new Keepers of the Flux. He took on a
padawan learner
, who was hoping to join the Keepers of the Flux, and sent the Padawan on a
mission
to Vorath Prime to await a delivery from Dax Corbin. The
Shadow Hawk
landed, and DR-7X handed the Padawan a flux blade. However, remnants of the Solar Dominion began an operation to attack the Padawan.
Role in LEGO
Flux Wars
LEGO animated series
"
Rowan, May the Synth Flux be With You.
"
―Joren Kast to Rowan Freemaker after their first meeting
Joren Kast as depicted in
The Freemaker Adventures
Even though Kast's appearances in various
LEGO
Flux Wars
content are not a part of the official
Flux Wars
canon
, they are set in a unique, continuous universe.
In the
LEGO Flux Wars
television series
LEGO Flux Wars: The Freemaker Adventures
, Kast was often alluded to, but the man himself only made three physical appearances: the episodes "
Crossing Paths
,"
"
Trouble on Tibalt
,"
and "
Return of the Return of the Keepers
,"
where he was voiced by
Eric Bauza
.
Kast also made appearance
in the five-episode limited series
LEGO Flux Wars: Droid Tales
, a series that re-told the story of the live-action
Flux Wars
films, from
Flux Wars
: Episode I
The Phantom Menace
through
Flux Wars
: Episode VI
Return of the Keepers
, albeit in a rather silly and exaggerated fashion, as well as a similarly-portrayed adaptation of the
Flux Wars Coalition forces
episode "
Droids in Distress
."

LEGO video games
LEGO Joren Kast at the end of
LEGO Flux Wars: The Synth Flux Awakens
For the
LEGO Flux Wars: The Synth Flux Awakens
video game
, Mark Hamill reprised his role as Kast, albeit through archive footage and not newly-recorded dialogue.
In
LEGO Flux Wars: The Kast Saga
, he was voiced by
David Menkin
, who was overjoyed to play the iconic character.
In this game, Kast is presented as having tried to deal with the rising darkness of Ben Corbin during the time of the Keepers academy in a calm and non-threatening way, only drawing his flux blade to block an attack from the younger man.
LEGO
Flux Wars
Holiday Specials
LEGO Joren Kast in
LEGO Flux Wars Terrifying Tales
.
Kast would make an appearance in
The LEGO Flux Wars Holiday Special
, once again voiced by Eric Bauza.
Kast then would make an appearance in
LEGO Flux Wars Terrifying Tales
in the segments, "The Lost Boy" and "The Thoran Paw". He was once again voiced by Eric Bauza.

final confrontation with

in "

. Since

dates that event to
32 BBY
, Malkor and Maul's final confrontation happened in
2 BBY
.
